Unit 1 — Number Systems & Representation · 100 solved questions (2026-10-26)
base-conversions 29
DL-U1-Q01MCQeasy

The octal number (756)8 is equal to which one of the following binary strings?

  1. 111101110
  2. 111110110
  3. 111101100
  4. 111011110
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Base 8 and base 2 are linked by 8 = 23, so each octal digit is replaced by exactly three bits, independently of its neighbours.

Step by step.

  1. Split the octal number into its digits: 7, 5, 6.
  2. Convert each digit on its own: 7 = 111, 5 = 101, 6 = 110.
  3. Concatenate without dropping zeros: 111 101 110.
  4. Check through decimal: (756)8 = 7×64 + 5×8 + 6 = 448 + 40 + 6 = 494, and 1111011102 = 256 + 128 + 64 + 32 + 8 + 4 + 2 = 494.

Answer: 1111011102

Common misconception. Converting through decimal and then writing the binary of each digit separately, which silently introduces decimal-to-binary errors; or grouping in four bits, which is the rule for hexadecimal, not octal.

Takeaway. Fix the group width before you start: 3 bits per octal digit, 4 bits per hex digit, and never drop a leading zero inside a group.

DL-U1-Q02MCQeasy

What is the minimum number of bits needed to represent the decimal number 1000 as an unsigned binary integer?

  1. 9
  2. 10
  3. 11
  4. 12
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. An unsigned binary number with n bits spans 0 to 2n − 1, so the requirement is 2n − 1 ≥ N, that is n ≥ ⌈log2(N + 1)⌉.

Step by step.

  1. Compute the powers around 1000: 29 = 512 and 210 = 1024.
  2. Nine bits reach only 511, which is below 1000.
  3. Ten bits reach 1023, and 1000 ≤ 1023, so ten bits suffice.

Answer: 10 bits

Common misconception. Answering 9 because 29 = 512 feels close to 1000, or using log10 instead of log2.

Takeaway. Count bits by the largest value the width can hold, not by the first power that looks large enough.

DL-U1-Q07MSQeasy

Which of the following represent the decimal value 255? (Select all that apply.)

  1. 0xFF in hexadecimal
  2. (377)8 in octal
  3. 11111111 in binary
  4. 0x1FF in hexadecimal
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. The same quantity has one representation per base, so each option must be converted to decimal and compared with 255.

Step by step.

  1. 0xFF = 15×16 + 15 = 255. True.
  2. (377)8 = 3×64 + 7×8 + 7 = 192 + 56 + 7 = 255. True.
  3. 111111112 = 28 − 1 = 255. True.
  4. 0x1FF = 1×256 + 15×16 + 15 = 511. False.

Answer: Options (A), (B) and (C) only.

Common misconception. Treating 0x1FF as "FF plus a bit" and assuming it is still 255; in hexadecimal each extra digit multiplies the value by 16.

Takeaway. 0xFF is the largest 8-bit value; adding a hex digit pushes the number into the 9-bit range.

DL-U1-Q09NATeasy

Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Each bit contributes 2 raised to its position, counting positions from 0 at the right.

Step by step.

  1. Positions with a 1 are 6, 5, 3, 1 and 0.
  2. Add 26 + 25 + 23 + 21 + 20 = 64 + 32 + 8 + 2 + 1.
  3. The total is 107.

Answer: 107

Common misconception. Counting bit positions from the left, which turns 1101011 into 1 + 2 + 8 + 32 + 64 with the wrong weights.

Takeaway. Write the place values above the bits before adding; seven bits always end at 26 = 64.

DL-U1-Q11MCQmedium

The binary fraction (0.1101)2 equals which decimal value?

  1. 0.8125
  2. 0.6875
  3. 0.875
  4. 0.625
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Bits to the right of the point carry the weights 2−1, 2−2, 2−3, and so on, so the digits are summed as fractions, not as integers.

Step by step.

  1. 1 × 2−1 = 0.5
  2. 1 × 2−2 = 0.25
  3. 0 × 2−3 = 0
  4. 1 × 2−4 = 0.0625
  5. Sum: 0.5 + 0.25 + 0 + 0.0625 = 0.8125.

Answer: 0.8125

Common misconception. Reading the four bits as the integer 13 and writing 0.13, or reversing the weight order and getting 0.6875.

Takeaway. Fractional bits halve as they move right; four bits can express only multiples of 1/16.

DL-U1-Q12MCQmedium

In which base b is the numeral (121)b equal to the decimal number 100?

  1. 9
  2. 8
  3. 10
  4. 11
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. A positional numeral is evaluated by expanding its digits against powers of the base, which turns the question into an equation in b.

Step by step.

  1. Expand: (121)b = 1·b2 + 2·b + 1.
  2. Recognise the perfect square: b2 + 2b + 1 = (b + 1)2.
  3. Set (b + 1)2 = 100, so b + 1 = 10 and b = 9.
  4. Verify: (121)9 = 81 + 18 + 1 = 100.

Answer: b = 9

Common misconception. Trying bases by eye and stopping at 10 because 121 looks familiar in decimal; the digit 2 is legal in every base above 2, so legality alone does not identify the base.

Takeaway. Whenever the digits form a symmetric pattern, look for a perfect-square or perfect-cube structure before expanding.

DL-U1-Q18MSQmedium

Which of the following conversions are correct? (Select all that apply.)

  1. 0x2F = 47
  2. (101110)2 = 46
  3. (64)8 = 52
  4. 0x100 = 255
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Every claim of equality between bases is settled by expanding the numeral in powers of its own base.

Step by step.

  1. 0x2F = 2×16 + 15 = 47. Correct.
  2. (101110)2 = 32 + 8 + 4 + 2 = 46. Correct.
  3. (64)8 = 6×8 + 4 = 52. Correct.
  4. 0x100 = 1×256 = 256, not 255. Incorrect.

Answer: Options (A), (B) and (C) only.

Common misconception. Treating 0x100 as "the maximum three-digit hex value"; the maximum is 0xFF = 255, and 0x100 is one more.

Takeaway. A new digit position in base b multiplies the value by b, so 0x100 is 16 times 0x10.

DL-U1-Q25NAThard

Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. A base-7 numeral is expanded against powers of 7, and the digit set is 0 to 6, so every digit here is legal.

Step by step.

  1. Positions from the right: 6×70, 2×71, 0×72, 2×73.
  2. Compute the powers: 70 = 1, 71 = 7, 72 = 49, 73 = 343.
  3. Sum: 2×343 + 0×49 + 2×7 + 6 = 686 + 0 + 14 + 6.
  4. The total is 706.

Answer: 706

Common misconception. Reading the numeral as decimal because the digits look familiar, or using powers of 10 for the higher positions.

Takeaway. Write the powers of the base first; a numeral's value depends only on its digits and its base, never on how it looks.

DL-U1-S2-Q01MCQeasy

The hexadecimal number 0xB7C equals which decimal value?

  1. 2940
  2. 2936
  3. 2844
  4. 2952
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Each hexadecimal digit is weighted by a power of 16, so the value is 11×162 + 7×161 + 12×160.

Step by step.

  1. B = 11, 7 = 7, C = 12.
  2. 11 × 256 = 2816.
  3. 7 × 16 = 112.
  4. 12 × 1 = 12.
  5. 2816 + 112 + 12 = 2940.

Answer: 2940

Common misconception. Reading 0xB7C as if the letters were decimal digits, or weighting from the wrong end (12×256 instead of 11×256).

DL-U1-S2-Q02MCQeasy

The binary number (1101.101)2 equals which decimal value?

  1. 13.625
  2. 13.5
  3. 13.75
  4. 13.125
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. The integer part uses place values 8, 4, 2, 1; the fractional part uses 1/2, 1/4, 1/8.

Step by step.

  1. Integer part: 1101 = 8 + 4 + 0 + 1 = 13.
  2. Fractional part: 101 = 1/2 + 0/4 + 1/8 = 0.5 + 0.125 = 0.625.
  3. Total = 13.625.

Answer: 13.625

Common misconception. Treating the fractional bits as decimal digits (0.101 read as 0.101 decimal), or weighting the fraction left to right as 1/8, 1/4, 1/2.

DL-U1-S2-Q03MCQmedium

The octal sum (27)8 + (45)8 expressed in octal is

  1. (74)8
  2. (72)8
  3. (64)8
  4. (714)8
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Octal digits are added with a carry of 1 whenever a column reaches 8, exactly as decimal addition carries at 10.

Step by step.

  1. Units column: 7 + 5 = 12 = 1×8 + 4, so write 4 and carry 1.
  2. Eights column: 2 + 4 + 1 = 7, which is below 8, so write 7 with no carry.
  3. Result: (74)8.
  4. Check in decimal: 23 + 37 = 60, and (74)8 = 7×8 + 4 = 60.

Answer: (74)8

Common misconception. Carrying at 10 out of habit gives (72)8 or (64)8; the carry point is the base, not the decimal ten.

DL-U1-S2-Q04MCQeasy

The binary string 1101 0110 1111 written in hexadecimal is

  1. 0xD6F
  2. 0xD5F
  3. 0xED6
  4. 0xB6F
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Hexadecimal and binary are linked by 16 = 24, so the bits are grouped in fours starting from the binary point.

Step by step.

  1. Group: 1101 | 0110 | 1111.
  2. 1101 = 13 = D.
  3. 0110 = 6.
  4. 1111 = 15 = F.
  5. Result: 0xD6F.

Answer: 0xD6F

Common misconception. Grouping from the left when the bit count is not a multiple of four, or dropping a leading zero inside a group and shifting every later digit.

DL-U1-S2-Q05MSQmedium

Which of the following statements about base conversion are TRUE? (Select all that apply.)

  1. Every octal digit expands to exactly three binary bits.
  2. Every hexadecimal digit expands to exactly four binary bits.
  3. Binary-to-octal conversion groups the bits in fours.
  4. For any base b > 1, the numeral (1000)b equals b3.
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Conversion between base 2 and base 2k is mechanical because each digit of the larger base is exactly k bits.

Step by step.

  1. (A) 8 = 23, so one octal digit is three bits, including leading zeros inside the group: 1 = 001. True.
  2. (B) 16 = 24, so one hexadecimal digit is four bits: A = 1010. True.
  3. (C) Octal needs groups of three, not four; fours belong to hexadecimal. False.
  4. (D) In base b the numeral 1000 has value 1×b3 + 0 + 0 + 0 = b3. True.

Answer: (A), (B) and (D)

Common misconception. Option (C) is the standard slip: the group width must match the power of two that the base is, so three bits for octal and four for hexadecimal.

DL-U1-S2-Q06NATeasy

Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Convert each operand to decimal first, or add in binary and then convert; both routes must agree.

Step by step.

  1. (101101)2 = 32 + 8 + 4 + 1 = 45.
  2. (1101)2 = 8 + 4 + 1 = 13.
  3. 45 + 13 = 58.
  4. Check in binary: 101101 + 001101 = 111010 = 32 + 16 + 8 + 2 = 58.

Answer: 58

Common misconception. Aligning the operands on the left instead of the right, which turns 1101 into 110100 and gives a sum far too large.

DL-U1-S2-Q07NAThard

Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. The fractional digits of a base-16 numeral are weighted by 1/16, 1/256, and so on, just as decimal fractions are weighted by 1/10, 1/100.

Step by step.

  1. Integer part: 2×16 + 14 = 32 + 14 = 46.
  2. Fractional part: A = 10, so 10/16 = 0.625.
  3. Total = 46.625.

Answer: 46.625

Common misconception. Reading the fraction as 0.A in decimal, i.e. 0.1; a single hex digit after the point has denominator 16, not 10.

DL-U1-Q51MCQeasy

The hexadecimal numeral (5C7)16 equals which octal numeral?

  1. (2070)8
  2. (1307)8
  3. (2707)8
  4. (2750)8
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Hexadecimal and octal both ride on binary: one hex digit is exactly four bits and one octal digit is exactly three bits, so the conversion goes hex → binary → regroup in threes from the right.

Step by step.

  1. Expand each hex digit into four bits: 5 = 0101, C = 1100, 7 = 0111, giving 0101 1100 0111.
  2. Regroup that string in threes, starting at the right-hand end: 010 | 111 | 000 | 111.
  3. Read each group: 010 = 2, 111 = 7, 000 = 0, 111 = 7, so the answer is (2707)8.
  4. Check through decimal: (5C7)16 = 5×256 + 12×16 + 7 = 1280 + 192 + 7 = 1479, and (2707)8 = 2×512 + 7×64 + 0×8 + 7 = 1024 + 448 + 7 = 1479.

Answer: (2707)8, option (C).

Common misconception. Regrouping from the left instead of the right pads the wrong end and yields (2070)8, the most popular wrong option here. Groups of four (the hex grouping) applied to an octal answer produce (1307)8.

DL-U1-Q52MCQeasy

The base-5 numeral (243)5 equals which decimal value?

  1. 63
  2. 73
  3. 83
  4. 53
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. A numeral in base b is a weighted sum: the digit in position k from the right carries the weight bk.

Step by step.

  1. Write the weights for a three-digit base-5 numeral: 52 = 25, 51 = 5, 50 = 1.
  2. Multiply digit by weight: 2×25 = 50, 4×5 = 20, 3×1 = 3.
  3. Add: 50 + 20 + 3 = 73.

Answer: 73, option (B).

Common misconception. Reading the digits as if they were decimal gives 243; using powers of 10 in place of powers of 5 gives 2×100 + 4×10 + 3 = 243 as well. The base sets the weights, not the digit values.

DL-U1-Q53MCQmedium

In base 5, the product (34)5 × (2)5 expressed in base 5 is

  1. (113)5
  2. (68)5
  3. (130)5
  4. (123)5
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Arithmetic in a base other than ten works digit by digit with a carry of b, not of 10; an answer written in base 5 may contain only the digits 0 to 4.

Step by step.

  1. Digit-wise: 4×2 = 8, which is 1×5 + 3, so write 3 and carry 1.
  2. 3×2 = 6, plus the carry 1 gives 7, which is 1×5 + 2, so write 2 and carry 1.
  3. The final carry 1 becomes the leading digit: (123)5.
  4. Check in decimal: (34)5 = 3×5 + 4 = 19 and 19×2 = 38; and (123)5 = 25 + 10 + 3 = 38.

Answer: (123)5, option (D).

Common misconception. Option (68)5 is the decimal product with a base-5 subscript attached; the digit 8 does not even exist in base 5. Option (130)5 comes from carrying 1 at 5 instead of at 5 in the correct column.

DL-U1-Q54MCQeasy

Which of the following numerals has the largest value?

  1. (1001)2
  2. (21)5
  3. (13)7
  4. (14)8
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Numerals in different bases can only be compared after both are converted to a common base, usually ten.

Step by step.

  1. (1001)2 = 8 + 1 = 9.
  2. (21)5 = 2×5 + 1 = 11.
  3. (13)7 = 1×7 + 3 = 10.
  4. (14)8 = 1×8 + 4 = 12.
  5. Comparing 9, 11, 10 and 12, the largest is 12, that is (14)8.

Answer: (14)8, option (D).

Common misconception. Assuming the numeral with the most digits, or with the largest base, must be the largest. Here the four-digit binary numeral is the smallest of the four.

DL-U1-Q55MSQmedium

Which of the following numerals equal the decimal number 200? (Select all that apply.)

  1. (1100 1000)2
  2. (310)8
  3. 0xC8
  4. (240)5
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Every numeral is a weighted sum in its own base, so the test is a conversion of each option to decimal.

Step by step.

  1. (1100 1000)2 = 128 + 64 + 8 = 200. Correct.
  2. (310)8 = 3×64 + 1×8 + 0 = 192 + 8 = 200. Correct.
  3. 0xC8 = 12×16 + 8 = 192 + 8 = 200. Correct.
  4. (240)5 = 2×25 + 4×5 + 0 = 50 + 20 = 70, not 200. Wrong.

Answer: options (A), (B) and (C).

Common misconception. Treating (240)5 as if the digits were decimal, or assuming that a numeral written in a small base must be the largest of the four. The base-5 item is in fact the smallest value listed.

DL-U1-Q56NATmedium

Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Mixed-base arithmetic is done by converting each numeral to one common base, here decimal, and then applying ordinary arithmetic.

Step by step.

  1. (2A7)16 = 2×256 + 10×16 + 7 = 512 + 160 + 7 = 679.
  2. (1111 0110)2 = 128 + 64 + 32 + 16 + 4 + 2 = 246.
  3. (507)8 = 5×64 + 0×8 + 7 = 320 + 7 = 327.
  4. 679 + 246 = 925, and 925 − 327 = 598.

Answer: 598.

Common misconception. Converting (2A7)16 as 2×100 + 10×10 + 7, which gives 307 and a final answer of 226. Each base supplies its own place values.

DL-U1-Q57NAThard

Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. A numeral with equal digits in base b is a polynomial in b, so recovering the base means solving a polynomial equation and keeping only the integer root that exceeds every digit used.

Step by step.

  1. Expand: (444)b = 4b2 + 4b + 4.
  2. Set the expansion equal to the given value: 4b2 + 4b + 4 = 364.
  3. Divide by 4: b2 + b + 1 = 91, so b2 + b − 90 = 0.
  4. Factor: (b + 10)(b − 9) = 0, giving b = 9 or b = −10.
  5. A base must be an integer greater than every digit, so b = 9. Check: 4×81 + 4×9 + 4 = 324 + 36 + 4 = 364.

Answer: b = 9.

Common misconception. Dividing 364 by 3 and calling the result the base, or accepting the negative root. The constraint b > 4 (every digit must be legal) is part of the problem, and it also rules out b = 4.

DL-U1-D-Q01MCQeasy

The base-7 numeral (135)7 equals which decimal value?

  1. 75
  2. 85
  3. 63
  4. 96
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. A positional numeral is a weighted sum: the digit in position k, counting from 0 at the right-hand digit, is multiplied by bk.

Step by step.

  1. Write the place values for base 7: 72 = 49, 71 = 7, 70 = 1.
  2. Multiply each digit by its place value: 1×49 + 3×7 + 5×1.
  3. Add: 49 + 21 + 5 = 75.
  4. Check by dividing back: 75 = 1×49 + 26, and 26 = 3×7 + 5, so the digits are 1, 3, 5 again.

Answer: Option (A), 75.

Common misconception. Reading the digits with decimal weights (1×100 + 3×10 + 5 = 135) or starting the running multiply at 7 instead of 1, which gives 7×7 + 3×7 + 5 = 75 by coincidence of the wrong method only in some cases.

Takeaway. Write the place values above the digits before multiplying; the leftmost place value of an n-digit numeral in base b is bn−1.

DL-U1-D-Q02MCQeasy

How many bits are needed to write the octal numeral (777)8 in binary?

  1. 8
  2. 9
  3. 10
  4. 12
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Octal and binary are linked by 8 = 23, so every octal digit expands to exactly three bits, independently of its neighbours.

Step by step.

  1. Count the octal digits: 7, 7, 7 — three digits.
  2. Each digit takes three bits, so 3 × 3 = 9 bits are needed.
  3. Expand to confirm: 7 = 111, so (777)8 = 111 111 1112, nine bits, with no leading zero to drop.
  4. Cross-check in decimal: (777)8 = 7×64 + 7×8 + 7 = 511, and 511 = 29 − 1, which needs exactly nine bits.

Answer: Option (B), 9 bits.

Common misconception. Grouping octal digits in fours, the rule for hexadecimal, which would give 12 bits; or assuming the leading group can be shortened when the value is small, which is not the case here because 7 is the widest octal digit.

Takeaway. Bits = 3 × (number of octal digits) unless the leading digit is below 4, in which case the leading group is written with fewer bits only when leading zeros are dropped.

DL-U1-D-Q03MCQeasy

The binary numeral (110 011.101)2, written in octal, is

  1. (36.5)8
  2. (71.5)8
  3. (63.5)8
  4. (63.625)8
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Convert binary to octal by regrouping in threes, starting at the radix point and moving outward in both directions.

Step by step.

  1. Integer part 110 011 is already grouped in threes from the point: 110 = 6, 011 = 3, so the integer part is 63.
  2. Fraction part 101 is one group of three: 101 = 5, so the fraction is .5.
  3. The octal numeral is (63.5)8.
  4. Check through decimal: (110 011.101)2 = 32 + 16 + 2 + 1 + 0.5 + 0.125 = 51.625, and (63.5)8 = 6×8 + 3 + 5/8 = 48 + 3 + 0.625 = 51.625.

Answer: Option (C), (63.5)8.

Common misconception. Writing the binary fraction .101 straight across as the octal fraction .101 or as .625, which treats three binary digits as three octal digits; and regrouping the integer part from the left instead of from the radix point.

Takeaway. Group in threes outward from the radix point; pad the fraction on the right and the integer part on the left, never in the middle.

DL-U1-D-Q11MCQmedium

For which base b is the numeral (144)b equal to the decimal number 100?

  1. 7
  2. 9
  3. 8
  4. 12
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Evaluate the numeral as a polynomial in b and solve for b; the answer must also satisfy every digit < b.

Step by step.

  1. (144)b = 1·b2 + 4·b + 4 = 100.
  2. Rearrange: b2 + 4b − 96 = 0.
  3. Factor: (b + 12)(b − 8) = 0, so b = 8 (a base cannot be negative).
  4. Check: 64 + 32 + 4 = 100, and the digit 4 < 8, so the numeral is legal in base 8.

Answer: Option (C), b = 8.

Common misconception. Trying the bases by eye and accepting b = 9 because (121)9 = 100, a different numeral; or accepting the negative root because the arithmetic ‘works’.

Takeaway. Always solve the polynomial, then discard any root smaller than the largest digit used.

DL-U1-D-Q16MSQmedium

Which of the following statements about positional numerals are TRUE? (Select all that apply.)

  1. In base b the numeral (11)b has the value b + 1.
  2. Every non-negative integer has exactly one base-b representation that uses no leading zeros.
  3. In base b the permitted digits are 0 through b.
  4. The decimal fraction 0.5 is written (0.1)2 in binary.
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. A base-b positional system uses digits 0 to b−1, weights bk, and gives a unique normalised representation to every non-negative integer.

Step by step.

  1. (A): (11)b = 1·b + 1 = b + 1. True for every b ≥ 2.
  2. (B): the representation is the remainder sequence of repeated division by b, read upwards, which is unique once leading zeros are forbidden. True.
  3. (C): the digit b is not available — base b has exactly b digits, 0 to b − 1. False; (10)b is the numeral that carries the value b.
  4. (D): (0.1)2 = 1·2−1 = 0.5. True.

Answer: Options (A), (B) and (D).

Common misconception. Accepting (C) because base 10 is imagined to run 0–10; the digit 9 is the largest in base 10, and in general b is written as 10 in base b, never as a single digit.

Takeaway. Digits run 0 … b−1; the value of a fraction digit at position −k is b−k, so (0.1)b = 1/b.

DL-U1-D-Q21NATmedium

Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. The digit in position k of a base-3 numeral carries the weight 3k.

Step by step.

  1. Place values from the right: 30 = 1, 31 = 3, 32 = 9, 33 = 27.
  2. Multiply digit by weight: 2×27 + 1×9 + 0×3 + 2×1.
  3. Add: 54 + 9 + 0 + 2 = 65.
  4. Check by repeated division: 65 = 2×27 + 11, 11 = 1×9 + 2, 2 = 0×3 + 2, giving 2, 1, 0, 2.

Answer: 65

Common misconception. Using powers of 2 because the digits happen to be 0, 1 and 2 only; the base is fixed by the subscript, not by the digit set actually used.

Takeaway. Read the subscript first, then write the place values. Ternary digits look binary but weigh 3k.

DL-U1-D-Q23NATmedium

Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. To convert to base b, divide repeatedly by b and read the remainders bottom-up; equivalently, take the largest power of b that fits at each position.

Step by step.

  1. The powers of 9 are 1, 9, 81, 729, 6561 … The largest that fits in 1000 is 729 = 93, so there are four digits.
  2. 1000 − 1×729 = 271.
  3. 271 ÷ 81 = 3 remainder 28, so the second digit is 3.
  4. 28 ÷ 9 = 3 remainder 1, so the third digit is 3 and the last digit is 1.
  5. The numeral is (1331)9; check: 729 + 243 + 27 + 1 = 1000.

Answer: 1331

Common misconception. Using 8 as the base because 1000 = 83 + … looks tempting, which gives (1750)8; the base is 9, so the weights are 729, 81, 9 and 1.

Takeaway. Powers of the base decide the digit count: 93 ≤ 1000 < 94, so exactly four base-9 digits.

ones-twos-complement 25
DL-U1-Q03MCQeasy

Which of the following is the 12-bit two's complement representation of the decimal number −21?

  1. 1111 1110 1011
  2. 1111 1110 1010
  3. 1000 0001 0101
  4. 1111 1111 1011
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Two's complement negation is invert-all-bits then add 1, and the width of the field must be fixed before the inversion.

Step by step.

  1. Write +21 in 12 bits: 0000 0001 0101.
  2. Invert every bit: 1111 1110 1010.
  3. Add 1: 1111 1110 1011.
  4. Check as an unsigned value: 1111 1110 1011 = 4096 − 21 = 4075, and 4075 − 4096 = −21.

Answer: 1111 1110 10112

Common misconception. Stopping at the inversion, which gives the one's complement (option B), or setting only the sign bit, which is sign-magnitude (option C).

Takeaway. This is a variation of GATE CSE 2019, Question 4 (−28 in 16-bit two's complement, official key option C). The width and the magnitude changed; the invert-and-add-one method did not.

DL-U1-Q04MCQeasy

The 8-bit two's complement pattern 1111 0000 represents which decimal integer?

  1. −16
  2. −15
  3. −112
  4. +240
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. For an n-bit pattern whose leading bit is 1, the two's complement value is the unsigned value minus 2n.

Step by step.

  1. Read the pattern as unsigned: 1111 0000 = 240.
  2. The leading bit is 1, so subtract 28 = 256: 240 − 256 = −16.
  3. Cross-check by negating: invert 1111 0000 to 0000 1111, add 1 to get 0001 0000 = 16, so the original is −16.

Answer: −16

Common misconception. Reading the pattern as the unsigned value 240, or inverting without adding 1 and reporting −15, which is the one's complement reading.

Takeaway. This is a variation of GATE CSE 2016, Set 1, Question 7 (the 16-bit pattern 1111 1111 1111 0101 has value −11). The subtraction of 2n is the whole method.

DL-U1-Q13MCQmedium

In 8-bit two's complement arithmetic, the operation 0110 1001 − 1101 0110 produces which result, and does signed overflow occur?

  1. 1001 0011, and overflow occurs
  2. 1001 0011, and no overflow occurs
  3. 0100 1011, and overflow occurs
  4. 0011 0111, and no overflow occurs
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Subtraction is implemented as addition of the two's complement of the subtrahend, and signed overflow is decided by the signs of the operands and the result, not by the carry out.

Step by step.

  1. Interpret the operands: 0110 1001 = 105 and 1101 0110 = 214 − 256 = −42.
  2. The operation is 105 − (−42) = 105 + 42 = 147.
  3. The 8-bit signed range is −128 to +127, and 147 lies outside it, so signed overflow occurs.
  4. The bit pattern produced is 147 − 256 = −109, that is 1001 0011.
  5. Consistency check: both operands were positive after negation, and the result has its sign bit set, which is exactly the overflow signature.

Answer: 1001 0011, with overflow

Common misconception. Assuming that subtracting a negative number cannot overflow, or reading the carry out of the sign bit as an overflow flag; that carry detects unsigned wraparound only.

Takeaway. Overflow is a property of the operands' signs: two like-signed operands producing an unlike-signed result.

DL-U1-Q14MCQmedium

In 8-bit one's complement arithmetic, what is the value of (−5) + (−3) after the end-around carry is applied?

  1. −8
  2. −7
  3. +8
  4. −9
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. One's complement addition adds the patterns and folds the carry out of the sign position back into the least significant bit; the result is a one's complement pattern, so a leading 1 means a negative number whose magnitude is the bitwise complement.

Step by step.

  1. −5 in 8-bit one's complement: 0000 0101 inverted = 1111 1010.
  2. −3: 0000 0011 inverted = 1111 1100.
  3. Add: 1111 1010 + 1111 1100 = 1 1111 0110, a 9-bit intermediate.
  4. Fold the carry back: 1111 0110 + 1 = 1111 0111.
  5. Read the pattern: leading bit 1, so negate by inverting: 0000 1000 = 8, giving −8.

Answer: −8

Common misconception. Dropping the end-around carry, which leaves 1111 0110 and gives −9; that is the classic one-bit error in one's complement.

Takeaway. One's complement needs the carry folded back; two's complement simply discards the carry. That difference is why two's complement is used in hardware.

DL-U1-Q17MSQmedium

Which of the following statements about n-bit two's complement numbers are TRUE? (Select all that apply.)

  1. The negation of a value is obtained by inverting all its bits and adding 1.
  2. The most negative value −2n−1 has no positive counterpart in the same width.
  3. −1 is represented as all ones.
  4. For every value x, the n-bit sum x + (−x) is 0 without overflow.
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Two's complement is a modular code: patterns are integers modulo 2n, with the upper half read as negative.

Step by step.

  1. Inverting and adding 1 computes the additive inverse modulo 2n, so statement A is true.
  2. The range is −2n−1 to 2n−1 − 1, which contains 2n−1 negatives but only 2n−1 − 1 positives, so −2n−1 is unmatched. Statement B is true.
  3. −1 is 2n − 1, whose n bits are all 1. Statement C is true.
  4. Take x = −2n−1. Its negation overflows, and the n-bit sum of the pattern with itself is 0 by wraparound but is not a valid result. Statement D is false.

Answer: Options (A), (B) and (C) only.

Common misconception. Believing every number has an opposite; the asymmetry of the range makes the most negative value its own negation.

Takeaway. The single exception at −2n−1 explains why negating the smallest value in a machine is a real and commonly hit bug.

DL-U1-Q22MCQhard

What is the minimum number of bits in a two's complement register that can hold every integer from −1000 to +1000?

  1. 10
  2. 11
  3. 12
  4. 13
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. An n-bit two's complement register holds −2n−1 to 2n−1 − 1, so both ends of the required interval must be checked.

Step by step.

  1. With n = 10 the range is −512 to 511, which cannot hold 1000.
  2. With n = 11 the range is −1024 to 1023, which contains both −1000 and +1000.
  3. So 11 bits are necessary and sufficient.

Answer: 11 bits

Common misconception. Choosing 10 because 210 = 1024 exceeds 1000; that argument ignores the sign bit, which halves the positive range.

Takeaway. Solve the inequality 2n−1 ≥ 1001, not 2n ≥ 1000.

DL-U1-Q23MCQhard

In an 8-bit two's complement system, the value stored in a register is 1000 0000. The negation operation (invert all bits, then add 1) is applied to it. What is the resulting pattern?

  1. 1000 0000, the same pattern
  2. 0000 0000, because the value is negated
  3. 0111 1111, the inverted pattern without the increment
  4. 1000 0001, because the operation adds one twice
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Negation is addition of the additive inverse modulo 2n; when the value is the most negative one, its inverse does not exist in the same width and the operation wraps onto itself.

Step by step.

  1. 1000 0000 has value −128, the most negative 8-bit two's complement number.
  2. Invert: 0111 1111.
  3. Add 1: 1000 0000.
  4. The pattern is unchanged and still decodes to −128, so the negation silently failed to change the value; +128 is not representable in 8 bits.

Answer: 1000 0000, unchanged

Common misconception. Assuming negation always flips the sign; it does so except at the single most negative value, where it overflows.

Takeaway. This is why the absolute value of the most negative integer is itself in C and Java; the fix is to widen before negating.

DL-U1-S2-Q08MCQeasy

The 8-bit one’s complement representation of the decimal number −37 is

  1. 1101 1010
  2. 1101 1011
  3. 0010 0101
  4. 1101 1001
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. One's complement negates by inverting every bit; two's complement would additionally add one.

Step by step.

  1. +37 in 8-bit binary is 0010 0101.
  2. Invert all eight bits: 1101 1010.
  3. That is the one's complement form of −37. Adding one would give 1101 1011, which is the two's complement form.

Answer: 1101 1010

Common misconception. Option 1101 1011 is the two's complement encoding of −37; the question asks for one's complement, so no +1 is applied.

DL-U1-S2-Q09MCQeasy

The 8-bit two’s complement pattern 1000 0001 represents which decimal integer?

  1. −127
  2. −128
  3. −1
  4. +129
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. In two's complement the sign bit carries weight −27 = −128 and every other bit carries its ordinary positive weight.

Step by step.

  1. Sign bit contributes −128.
  2. Remaining bits 000 0001 contribute +1.
  3. Value = −128 + 1 = −127.

Answer: −127

Common misconception. −128 is the pattern 1000 0000; adding the low bit to it gives −127, not −128.

DL-U1-S2-Q10MCQmedium

The 8-bit two’s complement value 1100 1011 is sign-extended to 16 bits. The result is

  1. 1111 1111 1100 1011
  2. 0000 0000 1100 1011
  3. 1100 1011 0000 0000
  4. 1111 1111 0011 0101
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Sign extension copies the sign bit into every new high-order position, which preserves the value because each copied 1 contributes −2k + 2k = 0 in the new width.

Step by step.

  1. The sign bit of 1100 1011 is 1, so the number is negative.
  2. Fill the eight new high bits with 1s.
  3. Result: 1111 1111 1100 1011.
  4. Check: the original 8-bit value is 203 − 256 = −53, and the sign-extended 16-bit pattern 0xFFCB is 65483 − 65536 = −53. The value is unchanged.

Answer: 1111 1111 1100 1011

Common misconception. Zero-filling (option B) is only correct for unsigned numbers; for a negative value it silently changes −53 into +203.

DL-U1-S2-Q11MCQmedium

How many distinct integers can a 4-bit one’s complement register represent?

  1. 15
  2. 16
  3. 14
  4. 17
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. One's complement has 2n patterns but two of them denote zero, so the number of distinct values is 2n − 1.

Step by step.

  1. The patterns range over −7 to +7 for four bits.
  2. Counting them: 7 negative values, 7 positive values and zero = 15.
  3. The patterns 0000 and 1111 both mean zero, which is why 16 patterns yield only 15 values.

Answer: 15

Common misconception. Answering 16 from the pattern count; the duplicated zero is exactly what distinguishes one's complement from two's complement.

DL-U1-S2-Q12MSQmedium

Which of the following statements about n-bit two’s complement numbers are TRUE? (Select all that apply.)

  1. The representable range is −2n−1 to 2n−1 − 1.
  2. Negating −2n−1 in n-bit two’s complement returns −2n−1 again.
  3. One’s complement has a unique representation for zero.
  4. The most significant bit carries the weight −2n−1.
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. The sign bit's negative weight is what makes two's complement asymmetric: one more negative value than positive ones.

Step by step.

  1. (A) The patterns run from −2n−1 up to 2n−1 − 1. True.
  2. (B) −2n−1 is the pattern 100…0; inverting gives 011…1 and adding one returns 100…0. True — it is its own negation.
  3. (C) One's complement has both 000…0 and 111…1 for zero. False.
  4. (D) That negative weight is the definition of the format. True.

Answer: (A), (B) and (D)

Common misconception. Option (B) looks impossible but follows from the asymmetry: the most negative value has no positive counterpart, so negation wraps onto itself.

DL-U1-S2-Q13NAThard

Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. A pattern whose leading bit is 1 has value (unsigned value) − 2n.

Step by step.

  1. 0xFF9C as an unsigned 16-bit number = 15×4096 + 15×256 + 9×16 + 12 = 65436.
  2. Since the sign bit is 1, subtract 216 = 65536.
  3. 65436 − 65536 = −100.
  4. Check by negation: 0xFF9C = 1111 1111 1001 1100; inverting gives 0000 0000 0110 0011 = 99; adding one gives 100 = +100, so the original is −100.

Answer: −100

Common misconception. Reporting 65436, the unsigned reading; the same bits mean different integers depending on the declared interpretation.

DL-U1-Q58MCQeasy

The 4-bit two’s complement pattern 1110 represents which decimal integer?

  1. −6
  2. −2
  3. +14
  4. −1
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. In two’s complement the leading bit carries the weight −2n−1 and every other bit carries its usual positive weight.

Step by step.

  1. For four bits the weights are −8, 4, 2, 1.
  2. The pattern 1110 has the bits 1, 1, 1, 0, so the value is −8 + 4 + 2 + 0 = −2.
  3. Cross-check by negating: invert 1110 to get 0001, add 1 to get 0010 = 2, so the original pattern is −2.

Answer: −2, option (B).

Common misconception. Reading the pattern as unsigned gives 14, which is what option (C) offers. The same bit string means 14 unsigned and −2 signed; the interpretation is a property of the data type, not of the bits.

DL-U1-Q59MCQeasy

Which 8-bit pattern is the two’s complement representation of −100?

  1. 1110 0100
  2. 1001 1011
  3. 1001 1100
  4. 0110 0100
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. For a negative value the two’s complement pattern is 2n − |x|, equivalently invert the positive pattern and add 1.

Step by step.

  1. Write +100 in 8 bits: 0110 0100.
  2. Invert every bit: 1001 1011.
  3. Add 1: 1001 1100.
  4. Check by weight: −128 + 16 + 8 + 4 = −100.

Answer: 1001 1100, option (C).

Common misconception. Stopping after the inversion gives 1001 1011, which is the one’s complement pattern of −100 and equals −101. Option (A) is what you get by forgetting to leave the sign bit alone when converting −28.

DL-U1-Q60MCQmedium

In 8-bit two’s complement arithmetic, the computation 25 − 37 produces which 8-bit pattern?

  1. 0000 1100
  2. 1111 0011
  3. 1111 0101
  4. 1111 0100
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Subtraction is addition of the two’s complement of the subtrahend, with the carry out of the sign bit discarded.

Step by step.

  1. 25 = 0001 1001 and 37 = 0010 0101.
  2. Negate 37: invert to 1101 1010, add 1 to get 1101 1011, which is −37.
  3. Add: 0001 1001 + 1101 1011 = 1 1111 0100. Drop the carry out.
  4. Read the result: 1111 0100 = −128 + 116 = −12, and 25 − 37 = −12.

Answer: 1111 0100, option (D).

Common misconception. Keeping the carry out and adding it back, as in one’s complement, produces 1111 0101, which is −11. Two’s complement discards that carry; only one’s complement performs the end-around carry.

DL-U1-Q61MCQmedium

The 6-bit two’s complement pattern 101101 represents which decimal integer?

  1. −19
  2. −18
  3. −37
  4. −45
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. A pattern whose leading bit is 1 denotes a negative value equal to its magnitude pattern taken with a minus sign; the magnitude is recovered by negating the whole pattern.

Step by step.

  1. Invert 101101 to get 010010.
  2. Add 1: 010011, which is 16 + 2 + 1 = 19.
  3. So 101101 is −19.
  4. Check by weight: −32 + 8 + 4 + 1 = −19.

Answer: −19, option (A).

Common misconception. Adding 1 to the pattern and then inverting gives −18, and treating the pattern as unsigned gives 45. Inversion comes first, then the increment.

DL-U1-Q62MSQmedium

Which of the following statements about shifting an n-bit two’s complement number are TRUE? (Select all that apply.)

  1. An arithmetic right shift by one position divides the value by 2 and rounds the result toward −∞.
  2. A logical right shift by one position divides the value by 2 for every value, positive or negative.
  3. A left shift by one position doubles the value modulo 2n.
  4. An arithmetic right shift leaves the all-ones pattern, which is −1, unchanged.
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. An arithmetic shift replicates the sign bit, a logical shift inserts zeros, and a left shift is multiplication by 2 modulo 2n.

Step by step.

  1. Arithmetic right shift of −3 (1111 1101) gives 1111 1110 = −2, and −3/2 rounded toward −∞ is −2. Statement A holds.
  2. Logical right shift of −3 gives 0111 1110 = 126, not −1 or −1.5. Statement B fails: a logical shift inserts 0 into the sign position and destroys the sign.
  3. A left shift moves every bit one place up, so the value doubles; any bit shifted past the top is lost, which is exactly reduction modulo 2n. Statement C holds.
  4. All ones shifted right arithmetically is all ones again, so −1 maps to −1. Statement D holds.

Answer: options (A), (C) and (D).

Common misconception. Believing the two right shifts agree on negative numbers. They agree only while the sign bit is 0; the arithmetic shift is the one that preserves the sign and the one a compiler emits for a signed division by 2.

DL-U1-Q63NAThard

Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. For a pattern whose leading bit is 1, the value is the pattern read as unsigned minus 2n; equivalently, negate the pattern to obtain its magnitude.

Step by step.

  1. Read the 12 bits as unsigned: 1111 1011 0110 = 2048 + 1024 + 512 + 256 + 128 + 32 + 16 + 4 + 2.
  2. Sum the powers present: 2048 + 1024 = 3072, + 512 = 3584, + 256 = 3840, + 128 = 3968, + 32 = 4000, + 16 = 4016, + 4 = 4020, + 2 = 4022.
  3. Subtract 212 = 4096: 4022 − 4096 = −74.
  4. Check by negation: invert to 0000 0100 1001, add 1 to get 0000 0100 1010 = 64 + 8 + 2 = 74, so the pattern is −74.

Answer: −74.

Common misconception. Subtracting 211 instead of 212, which gives 1974 and is what happens when the width is misread as 11 bits. The width of the register, not the position of the leading 1, sets the modulus.

DL-U1-D-Q04MCQeasy

The 8-bit two’s complement pattern 1110 0110 represents which decimal integer?

  1. −25
  2. −122
  3. 26
  4. −26
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. In an n-bit two’s complement pattern the leading bit carries the weight −2n−1; every other bit keeps its positive weight.

Step by step.

  1. The leading bit is 1, so the value is negative.
  2. Sum the weights: −128 + 64 + 32 + 4 + 2 = −26.
  3. Confirm by complementing: invert 1110 0110 to 0001 1001 = 25, add 1 to get 26, so the pattern is −26.
  4. A second check: 256 − 230 = 26, and the sign bit is 1, so the value is −26.

Answer: Option (D), −26.

Common misconception. Inverting without adding 1, which gives the one’s complement reading of −25, or ignoring the sign bit and reading 230 as unsigned.

Takeaway. Two quick routes for a negative pattern: −(2n − unsigned value), or read the leading weight as −2n−1 and add the rest.

DL-U1-D-Q05MCQeasy

Which of the following is the 8-bit two’s complement representation of −45?

  1. 1101 0010
  2. 1010 1101
  3. 1101 0011
  4. 0010 1101
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. To negate in two’s complement, write the magnitude in n bits, invert every bit, then add 1.

Step by step.

  1. +45 in 8 bits is 0010 1101.
  2. Invert every bit: 1101 0010.
  3. Add 1: 1101 0011.
  4. Check by weight: −128 + 64 + 16 + 2 + 1 = −45.

Answer: Option (C), 1101 0011.

Common misconception. Stopping after the inversion, which yields the one’s complement pattern 1101 0010 and a value of −44; or setting the sign bit on the magnitude, which gives 1010 1101 and a value of −83.

Takeaway. Invert then add 1 — and always check the result by weighting the bits, which catches a forgotten carry.

DL-U1-D-Q06MCQeasy

How many distinct integer values can an n-bit one’s complement register represent?

  1. 2n
  2. 2n − 1
  3. 2n + 1
  4. 2n−1
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. One’s complement has two representations of zero — all zeros and all ones — so the 2n patterns carry one value fewer.

Step by step.

  1. An n-bit register has 2n patterns.
  2. 000…0 and 111…1 both denote zero, so one pattern is wasted.
  3. The representable range is −(2n−1 − 1) to +(2n−1 − 1), a count of 2(2n−1 − 1) + 1 = 2n − 1.
  4. For n = 4 this gives −7 to +7, fifteen values, which agrees with the formula.

Answer: Option (B), 2n − 1.

Common misconception. Answering 2n by analogy with two’s complement, where every pattern is a distinct value; the double zero is the whole difference between the two systems.

Takeaway. One’s complement: 2n − 1 values, two zeros, range symmetric about zero. Two’s complement: 2n values, one zero, range asymmetric.

DL-U1-D-Q12MCQmedium

An 8-bit two’s complement adder computes 0111 0101 + 0010 1101. Which result pattern is produced, and does signed overflow occur?

  1. 1010 0010, and signed overflow occurs
  2. 1010 0010, and no signed overflow occurs
  3. 0010 0010, and signed overflow occurs
  4. 0010 0010, and no signed overflow occurs
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Signed overflow occurs when both operands share a sign and the result has the opposite sign; it is not the same flag as the carry out of the sign bit.

Step by step.

  1. Read the operands: 0111 0101 = 117, 0010 1101 = 45, both positive.
  2. 117 + 45 = 162, which is greater than the largest positive 8-bit value, 127.
  3. 162 − 256 = −94, whose pattern is 1010 0010 (128 + 32 + 2 = 162 as unsigned).
  4. The two positive operands produced a negative result, so signed overflow is flagged; the carry out of the sign bit is 0, which is why the carry flag alone would have missed it.

Answer: Option (A), 1010 0010 with signed overflow.

Common misconception. Watching the carry out of the most significant bit: here it is 0, so a candidate who uses it as the overflow flag reports no overflow on a sum that clearly overflows.

Takeaway. Overflow rule for addition: same signs in, different sign out. The carry out answers a different question, about unsigned arithmetic.

DL-U1-D-Q17MSQmedium

Which of the following statements about sign extension are TRUE? (Select all that apply.)

  1. Extending an 8-bit two’s complement number to 16 bits replicates bit 7 eight times.
  2. Sign extension changes the value of a two’s complement number.
  3. Zero-extending an 8-bit two’s complement pattern preserves its value exactly when the number is non-negative.
  4. In one’s complement a value is also extended by replicating its sign bit.
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Widening a signed number keeps its value only if the new high bits copy the old sign bit; zero-filling is correct only for unsigned or non-negative values.

Step by step.

  1. (A): the added bits must all equal the original sign bit, so eight copies of bit 7 are appended. True.
  2. (B): extension is defined to preserve the value; if the value changed the operation would be useless. False.
  3. (C): for a non-negative number the sign bit is 0, so zero-filling copies the sign bit and preserves the value; for a negative number zero-filling would turn it positive. True.
  4. (D): in one’s complement the sign bit is also the value’s sign marker, and replicating it preserves the value, including the negative-zero pattern. True.

Answer: Options (A), (C) and (D).

Common misconception. Thinking zero-extension is always safe because the added bits are ‘just zeros’; for a negative pattern those zeros destroy the sign and change −26 into 230.

Takeaway. Sign-extend for signed types, zero-extend for unsigned ones. The rule to remember is ‘copy the sign bit’, not ‘pad with zeros’.

DL-U1-D-Q22NATmedium

Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. For a pattern with a leading 1, the value is the leading weight −2n−1 plus the positive weights of the remaining 1 bits.

Step by step.

  1. The leading bit is 1, so the value is negative.
  2. Sum the weights of the set bits: −128 + 64 + 4 + 2 + 1.
  3. −128 + 71 = −57.
  4. Check by complementing: invert 1100 0111 to 0011 1000 = 56, add 1 to get 57, so the pattern is −57.

Answer: −57

Common misconception. Reading 1100 0111 as unsigned 199 and reporting a positive value, or stopping at the inversion and reporting −56.

Takeaway. −(2n − unsigned) is the fastest route: 256 − 199 = 57, and the sign bit makes it −57.

range-and-overflow 22
DL-U1-Q05MCQeasy

Which range of integers can be stored in a 12-bit two's complement register?

  1. −2048 to +2047
  2. −2047 to +2047
  3. −4096 to +4095
  4. 0 to 4095
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. An n-bit two's complement field holds −2n−1 through 2n−1 − 1, because zero occupies one of the 2n patterns and there is no negative zero.

Step by step.

  1. With n = 12, the most negative value is −211 = −2048.
  2. The most positive value is 211 − 1 = 2047.
  3. Total patterns: 2048 + 2047 + 1 (for zero) = 4096 = 212.

Answer: −2048 to +2047

Common misconception. Assuming the range is symmetric, which is true of sign-magnitude and one's complement but false of two's complement; option C is the unsigned range reinterpreted.

Takeaway. Two's complement is asymmetric by exactly one: the negative side holds one extra value.

DL-U1-Q10NATeasy

Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. The number of distinct values a field can hold is 2n; what changes between representations is which values those are.

Step by step.

  1. An 8-bit field has 28 = 256 distinct bit patterns.
  2. Two's complement assigns each pattern a different integer: −128 through −1, zero, and 1 through 127.
  3. 128 + 1 + 127 = 256, so no pattern is wasted and none is duplicated.

Answer: 256

Common misconception. Answering 255 on the assumption that two patterns encode zero; that waste belongs to sign-magnitude and one's complement, which two's complement removes.

Takeaway. Two's complement covers all 2n patterns, which is exactly why its range is one wider on the negative side.

DL-U1-Q15MCQmedium

An 8-bit unsigned adder receives 1100 1000 and 0110 0100. What does it produce, and what is the carry out?

  1. 0010 1100 with carry-out 1
  2. 0010 1100 with carry-out 0
  3. 300 with carry-out 0
  4. 0010 1101 with carry-out 1
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. An unsigned adder works modulo 2n; the sum beyond the field width leaves through the carry-out, and the field keeps the remainder.

Step by step.

  1. Convert: 1100 1000 = 200 and 0110 0100 = 100.
  2. Exact sum: 300.
  3. 300 = 256 + 44, so the carry-out is 1 and the 8-bit field holds 44.
  4. 44 in binary is 0010 1100.

Answer: 0010 1100 with carry-out 1

Common misconception. Writing the mathematical answer 300, which an 8-bit field cannot hold; or calling the carry-out "overflow", which in unsigned arithmetic is simply the carry.

Takeaway. Unsigned: carry-out is the ninth bit. Signed: overflow is a sign anomaly. They are different flags.

DL-U1-Q19NATmedium

Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Two's complement addition is addition modulo 2n; a result outside the representable range wraps by exactly 2n and the sign flips.

Step by step.

  1. The exact sum is 40000.
  2. The 16-bit signed range is −32768 to +32767, and 40000 exceeds it.
  3. Reduce modulo 65536: 40000 − 65536 = −25536.
  4. So the machine holds the pattern for −25536, which is 1001 1100 0100 0000.

Answer: −25536

Common misconception. Subtracting 32768 instead of 65536, which gives +7232; the modulus is the number of patterns, 216, not the magnitude of the most negative value.

Takeaway. When a signed sum overflows, the wrap amount is 2n, and the sign of the result flips from positive to negative.

DL-U1-Q24MSQhard

Which of the following statements about signed overflow in n-bit two's complement addition are TRUE? (Select all that apply.)

  1. Overflow occurs exactly when both operands have the same sign and the result has the opposite sign.
  2. Overflow cannot occur when the two operands have opposite signs.
  3. The carry out of the sign bit is by itself a correct signed overflow indicator.
  4. Overflow occurs exactly when the carry into the sign bit differs from the carry out of it.
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Signed overflow means the true sum lies outside −2n−1..2n−1−1; it is a statement about magnitudes, and it is equivalent to a mismatch between the two carries at the sign position.

Step by step.

  1. If the operands have the same sign, their magnitudes add; the sum can only leave the range on the far side, so the sign must flip. Statement A is true.
  2. With opposite signs the true sum lies strictly between the operands, hence inside the range. Statement B is true.
  3. The carry out of the sign bit is the unsigned carry; for example −1 + −1 produces a carry out of 1 while being perfectly valid, and 127 + 1 produces no carry out while overflowing. Statement C is false.
  4. Adding the carries into and out of the sign position: they agree exactly when no overflow occurred. Statement D is true.

Answer: Options (A), (B) and (D) only.

Common misconception. Confusing the unsigned carry flag with the signed overflow flag; hardware keeps them as two separate flags for exactly this reason.

Takeaway. The two-carry rule is the one a datapath can implement with a single XOR gate, and it is the definition to quote in an exam.

DL-U1-S2-Q20MCQeasy

A 6-bit unsigned binary number represents decimal values in the range

  1. 0 to 63
  2. 0 to 64
  3. 1 to 64
  4. 0 to 127
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. An n-bit unsigned field holds 2n patterns, numbered 0 to 2n − 1.

Step by step.

  1. 26 = 64 distinct patterns.
  2. The smallest is all zeros = 0.
  3. The largest is all ones = 63.
  4. Range: 0 to 63.

Answer: 0 to 63

Common misconception. 0 to 64 counts the patterns rather than the values; there are 64 values but the top one is 63.

DL-U1-S2-Q21MCQeasy

How many distinct bit patterns can a 10-bit register hold?

  1. 1024
  2. 1023
  3. 512
  4. 2048
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Each bit doubles the number of patterns, so n bits give exactly 2n.

Step by step.

  1. 210 = 1024.
  2. This count is independent of how the bits are interpreted: 1024 patterns whether read as unsigned, signed or as a floating point field.

Answer: 1024

Common misconception. 1023 is the largest unsigned value that fits, not the number of patterns.

DL-U1-S2-Q22MCQmedium

An 8-bit two’s complement adder computes 0111 1111 + 0000 0001. Which statement is correct?

  1. The result 1000 0000 is correct and equals +128.
  2. Signed overflow occurs: the true sum +128 lies outside the 8-bit range.
  3. No overflow occurs because the carry-out of the sign bit is 0.
  4. The result is correct once the carry-out is added back.
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Overflow in two's complement addition means the mathematical sum is outside the representable range, so the stored pattern denotes a different number.

Step by step.

  1. 0111 1111 = +127, the largest positive 8-bit two's complement value.
  2. Adding +1 gives the true sum +128.
  3. The stored pattern is 1000 0000, which as two's complement is −128.
  4. Adding two positive operands produced a negative result: that is exactly the overflow condition, and the carry into the sign bit is 1 while the carry out is 0.

Answer: Signed overflow occurs

Common misconception. The pattern 1000 0000 is a legal two's complement number (−128), so nothing looks wrong at the bit level; the fault only shows when the operands and the result are compared by sign.

DL-U1-S2-Q23MSQmedium

Which of the following statements about overflow are TRUE? (Select all that apply.)

  1. Overflow in unsigned addition is signalled by a carry-out of the most significant bit.
  2. In two’s complement addition, overflow cannot occur when the two operands have opposite signs.
  3. A two’s complement subtraction A − B can overflow only when A and B have opposite signs.
  4. Discarding the carry-out of an unsigned addition always leaves the correct result.
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Unsigned and signed overflow are different events: the carry flag answers one question and the overflow flag the other.

Step by step.

  1. (A) For unsigned numbers the sum exceeding 2n − 1 is exactly a carry out of the top bit. True.
  2. (B) Opposite signs give a sum of smaller magnitude, which always fits. True.
  3. (C) Subtraction A − B is A + (−B), and −B has the opposite sign to B, so an overflow needs A and B to differ in sign. True.
  4. (D) Discarding the carry loses 2n, which is precisely the amount by which the sum overflowed. False.

Answer: (A), (B) and (C)

Common misconception. Option (D) is the trap: dropping the carry is what the hardware does, but the result is then wrong, not merely unwrapped.

DL-U1-S2-Q24MSQmedium

Which of the following statements about the capacity of an n-bit register are TRUE? (Select all that apply.)

  1. The register holds 2n distinct bit patterns.
  2. The number of distinct values representable in n-bit two’s complement is 2n.
  3. In two’s complement, zero has two distinct representations.
  4. The value −2n−1 has no positive counterpart in n-bit two’s complement.
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Two's complement wastes no pattern: every one of the 2n patterns denotes a different value, which is why zero appears once and the negative end is one deeper than the positive end.

Step by step.

  1. (A) n independent bits give 2n patterns. True.
  2. (B) Because no pattern is wasted, 2n patterns mean 2n values. True.
  3. (C) Zero is the single pattern 000…0 in two's complement; the duplicate belongs to one's complement. False.
  4. (D) The positive value 2n−1 would need the pattern 100…0, which is already taken by −2n−1. True.

Answer: (A), (B) and (D)

Common misconception. Option (C) is the one's complement fact transplanted into two's complement, where the duplicated zero has been designed away.

DL-U1-S2-Q25NAThard

Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. An 8-bit adder works modulo 28; the stored pattern is the sum reduced mod 256, and reading it as two's complement reinterprets the same pattern.

Step by step.

  1. 200 + 200 = 400.
  2. 400 mod 256 = 144, which is 1001 0000 in binary.
  3. As an unsigned value that is 144; as two's complement, the sign bit is 1, so subtract 256.
  4. 144 − 256 = −112.

Answer: −112

Common misconception. Reporting 144 answers the unsigned reading; the question fixes the interpretation as two's complement, which turns the same bits into −112.

DL-U1-Q70MCQeasy

An 8-bit register holds the unsigned value 250. The value 10 is added and only the low 8 bits are kept. What is stored, and what is the carry-out of the most significant bit?

  1. 4, with carry-out 1
  2. 4, with carry-out 0
  3. 260, with carry-out 0
  4. 5, with carry-out 1
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. An n-bit register holds values modulo 2n; the carry-out of the top bit is the part that does not fit.

Step by step.

  1. 250 + 10 = 260.
  2. 260 − 256 = 4, so the stored byte is 0000 0100.
  3. Since 260 ≥ 256, the addition produced a carry-out of 1.
  4. Check in binary: 1111 1010 + 0000 1010 = 1 0000 0100.

Answer: 4 with carry-out 1, option (A).

Common misconception. Expecting the register to hold 260 or to saturate at 255. Unsigned hardware wraps silently and reports the wrap only through the carry flag.

DL-U1-Q71MCQeasy

Which of the following decimal values cannot be represented in a 4-bit two’s complement register?

  1. −8
  2. +7
  3. 0
  4. +8
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. An n-bit two’s complement register covers exactly 2n values, from −2n−1 to 2n−1 − 1.

Step by step.

  1. For n = 4 the range is −8 to +7.
  2. −8 is the pattern 1000 and +7 is 0111, both inside the range.
  3. 0 is 0000, also inside.
  4. +8 needs the pattern 1000, which is already −8, so +8 has no representation.

Answer: +8, option (D).

Common misconception. Assuming the range is symmetric, that is −8 to +8 or −7 to +7. The range is lopsided by one, which is why −8 has no positive counterpart in four bits.

DL-U1-Q72MCQmedium

In 8-bit two’s complement, exactly one of the following values overflows when it is doubled. Which one?

  1. 40
  2. 63
  3. 64
  4. −64
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Doubling is addition of a value to itself, so overflow occurs exactly when the doubled result leaves the representable range −128 to +127.

Step by step.

  1. 2 × 40 = 80, inside the range.
  2. 2 × 63 = 126, inside the range.
  3. 2 × 64 = 128, which exceeds the maximum +127, so the pattern 1000 0000 is produced and reads back as −128.
  4. 2 × (−64) = −128, which is exactly the minimum and is representable, so no overflow occurs.

Answer: 64, option (C).

Common misconception. Believing that the most negative value must overflow when doubled, or that 63 is the last safe value. The safe upper half ends at 63 and the safe lower half ends at −64, so the range is asymmetric.

DL-U1-Q73MSQmedium

Which of the following 8-bit unsigned additions produce a carry-out of the most significant bit? (Select all that apply.)

  1. 130 + 140
  2. 200 + 100
  3. 100 + 100
  4. 255 + 1
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. For unsigned operands an n-bit adder sets the carry-out exactly when the true sum is at least 2n; the stored byte is the sum reduced modulo 256.

Step by step.

  1. 130 + 140 = 270 ≥ 256, so carry-out 1 and stored value 14.
  2. 200 + 100 = 300 ≥ 256, so carry-out 1 and stored value 44.
  3. 100 + 100 = 200 < 256, so carry-out 0 and stored value 200.
  4. 255 + 1 = 256, so carry-out 1 and stored value 0.

Answer: options (A), (B) and (D).

Common misconception. Confusing the unsigned carry-out with signed overflow. Here the carry flag is the whole story; in signed arithmetic 130 and 140 are negative numbers and the same addition would set the overflow flag instead.

DL-U1-Q74MSQmedium

A 12-bit two’s complement adder adds the following pairs of operands. For which pairs does signed overflow occur? (Select all that apply.)

  1. 1500 + 1000
  2. −1500 + (−1000)
  3. 2000 + 100
  4. −2000 + (−48)
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. A 12-bit two’s complement register covers −2048 to +2047, and overflow means the true sum lies outside that interval.

Step by step.

  1. 1500 + 1000 = 2500 > 2047, so overflow.
  2. −1500 − 1000 = −2500 < −2048, so overflow.
  3. 2000 + 100 = 2100 > 2047, so overflow.
  4. −2000 − 48 = −2048, which is exactly the minimum representable value, so no overflow.

Answer: options (A), (B) and (C).

Common misconception. Rejecting pair (D) because both operands are large and negative, or accepting a sum that merely reaches the boundary. The endpoint −2048 is representable; only values strictly beyond it overflow.

DL-U1-Q75NAThard

Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. No overflow means the exact product must stay inside the representable interval −29 to 29 − 1, that is −512 to +511.

Step by step.

  1. The interval for 10-bit two’s complement is −512 to +511.
  2. For positive x the condition is 3x ≤ 511, so x ≤ 170.33, giving x = 170 at most.
  3. Check the largest candidate: 3 × 170 = 510, which is inside the interval.
  4. Check the next integer: 3 × 171 = 513 > 511, which overflows.
  5. For completeness the negative side is not binding: 3 × (−170) = −510 ≥ −512, so −170 is also safe, but the question asks for the largest x.

Answer: 170.

Common misconception. Dividing 511 by 3 and rounding to 170.33, or using 512 as the upper bound. The positive maximum is 2n−1 − 1 = 511, one less than the magnitude of the negative minimum.

DL-U1-D-Q10MCQeasy

Which of the following decimal values cannot be stored in an 8-bit unsigned register?

  1. 0
  2. 128
  3. 255
  4. 256
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. An n-bit unsigned register holds the 2n values 0 to 2n − 1.

Step by step.

  1. For n = 8 the range is 0 to 28 − 1 = 255.
  2. 0, 128 and 255 all lie inside that range.
  3. 256 needs nine bits (1 0000 0000), so it cannot be stored in eight.
  4. The register has 256 distinct patterns, but the top value is 255, not 256.

Answer: Option (D), 256.

Common misconception. Confusing the number of patterns (2n = 256) with the largest value (2n − 1 = 255), and concluding that 256 fits.

Takeaway. Range of an n-bit unsigned register: 0 … 2n − 1. The count of values and the maximum value differ by one.

DL-U1-D-Q15MCQhard

An 8-bit adder receives 1111 0001 and 0000 1111, and the result is read both as unsigned and as two’s complement. Which statement is correct?

  1. The carry-out is 1 and the overflow flag is 0; the stored result 0000 0000 is 0 under either reading.
  2. The carry-out is 1 and the overflow flag is 1, because the sign bit changed.
  3. The carry-out is 0 and the overflow flag is 1, because the sum wrapped.
  4. The carry-out is 0 and the overflow flag is 0; the stored result is 1111 1111.
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Carry-out and overflow are separate flags: carry-out reports that the unsigned sum exceeded 2n − 1, overflow reports that the signed sum left the two’s complement range.

Step by step.

  1. Unsigned reading: 241 + 15 = 256, so the stored 8 bits are 0000 0000 and the carry-out is 1.
  2. Signed reading: 1111 0001 = −15 and 0000 1111 = +15, whose sum is 0 with no overflow, because operands of opposite signs cannot overflow.
  3. Flag check by the XOR rule: the carry into the sign bit is 1 and the carry out of it is 1, so the overflow flag is 1 ⊕ 1 = 0.
  4. Both readings therefore give the value 0 from the stored pattern 0000 0000, while only the unsigned view needs the carry-out to know that 256 was produced.

Answer: Option (A), carry-out 1, overflow 0, result 0000 0000.

Common misconception. Assuming a carry-out of 1 implies signed overflow. Here the same addition wraps the unsigned sum past 255 yet is exactly correct in signed arithmetic, which is why the two flags are wired differently.

Takeaway. Carry-out = unsigned wrap. Overflow = XOR of the carry into and out of the sign bit. A single addition can set one, both or neither.

DL-U1-D-Q19MSQmedium

A 4-bit two’s complement system is used. Which of the following statements are TRUE? (Select all that apply.)

  1. The representable range is −8 to +7.
  2. The addition 0101 + 0100 produces signed overflow.
  3. The pattern 1000 denotes negative zero.
  4. The pattern 1111 denotes −7.
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. A 4-bit two’s complement register holds −23 to 23 − 1, has one zero, and every pattern is a distinct value.

Step by step.

  1. (A): the range is −2n−1 to 2n−1 − 1 = −8 to +7. True.
  2. (B): 0101 = 5 and 0100 = 4; 5 + 4 = 9 exceeds +7, and the stored pattern 1001 = −7. Two positive operands, negative result, so overflow. True.
  3. (C): 1000 = −8, the most negative value; two’s complement has a single zero, 0000. False.
  4. (D): 1111 = −1, not −7; the value −7 is 1001. False.

Answer: Options (A) and (B).

Common misconception. Carrying the one’s complement habit into two’s complement and expecting a negative zero; the extra pattern was spent on −8 instead, which is exactly why the range is asymmetric.

Takeaway. For n = 4: 16 patterns, values −8 to +7, one zero, and 1000 is the value with no positive counterpart.

DL-U1-D-Q20MSQhard

In an 8-bit two’s complement system the negation of a value is formed by inverting every bit and adding 1. Which of the following statements are TRUE? (Select all that apply.)

  1. Negating 0000 0000 returns 0000 0000.
  2. Negating 1000 0000 returns 1000 0000.
  3. For every value other than −128, negation produces a value of the opposite sign.
  4. Negating 0111 1111 returns 1000 0000.
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Negation is an involution except at the two ends of the range: zero maps to itself, and the most negative value maps to itself because its positive counterpart does not exist.

Step by step.

  1. (A): inverting 0000 0000 gives 1111 1111, and adding 1 gives 1 0000 0000, whose stored 8 bits are 0000 0000. True, with a carry out that is discarded.
  2. (B): inverting 1000 0000 gives 0111 1111, and adding 1 gives 1000 0000 again, so the operation returns the same pattern and sets the overflow flag. True.
  3. (C): for every other non-zero value the pattern is neither 0000 0000 nor 1000 0000, so inverting flips the sign bit and the increment cannot restore it; the result is the opposite sign. True.
  4. (D): inverting 0111 1111 gives 1000 0000, and adding 1 gives 1000 0001, which is −127. False.

Answer: Options (A), (B) and (C).

Common misconception. Forgetting the final increment on the boundary case and concluding that negating +127 gives −128; the correct result is −127, and −128 is reachable only from itself.

Takeaway. Negation fixes exactly two patterns, 0000 0000 and 1000 0000; at the second one the operation overflows silently, which is why −128 must be handled specially.

DL-U1-D-Q25NAThard

Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Keeping the low n bits of a result is arithmetic modulo 2n; the discarded high bits are the overflow.

Step by step.

  1. 3 × 200 = 600.
  2. The largest value the register can hold is 255, and 600 > 255, so the result wraps.
  3. Reduce modulo 256: 600 = 2×256 + 88.
  4. So the stored pattern is 88, and the 512 that was discarded sits above the register’s range.

Answer: 88

Common misconception. Subtracting 255 instead of 256, which gives 90 and treats the register as if it had 255 states rather than 256 patterns.

Takeaway. Low-bits arithmetic is mod 2n. Reduce with the modulus 2n, never with the maximum value 2n−1.

floating-point-ieee-754 24
DL-U1-Q06MCQeasy

In IEEE 754 single precision, the 8-bit exponent field is stored with which bias?

  1. 127
  2. 128
  3. 126
  4. 1023
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. IEEE 754 stores the exponent in excess-K form, where K = 2e−1 − 1 for an e-bit exponent field.

Step by step.

  1. Single precision uses an 8-bit exponent field, so the bias is 27 − 1 = 127.
  2. The stored field is the true exponent plus 127, so a stored 126 means the exponent is −1.
  3. Double precision uses an 11-bit field, whose bias is 210 − 1 = 1023.

Answer: 127

Common misconception. Using 128, the plain 27, which would shift every exponent by one; option D is the double-precision bias.

Takeaway. Bias = 2e−1 − 1, and the two reserved field values 0 and 255 are not ordinary exponents at all.

DL-U1-Q08MSQeasy

Which of the following statements about IEEE 754 formats are TRUE? (Select all that apply.)

  1. Single precision is laid out as 1 sign bit, 8 exponent bits and 23 fraction bits, 32 bits in all.
  2. The exponent is stored in excess-127 form.
  3. For normal numbers the leading 1 of the significand is not stored, giving an effective 24-bit significand.
  4. Double precision stores its exponent in an 8-bit field.
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. The IEEE 754 layout is a fixed field split whose widths decide precision and range; the significand is normalised so its leading bit need not be stored.

Step by step.

  1. Single precision: 1 + 8 + 23 = 32 bits. Statement A is true.
  2. The 8-bit exponent is biased by 127, so stored 127 means exponent 0. Statement B is true.
  3. Normalisation guarantees a leading 1, which is implied rather than stored, so 23 stored bits give 24 bits of significand. Statement C is true.
  4. Double precision is 1 + 11 + 52 = 64 bits, so its exponent field is 11 bits, not 8. Statement D is false.

Answer: Options (A), (B) and (C) only.

Common misconception. Believing the fraction field holds all the significand bits, or transferring the single-precision exponent width to double precision.

Takeaway. Learn the two layouts as the pairs (8, 23) and (11, 52); every other fact about IEEE 754 follows from them.

DL-U1-Q16MCQmedium

The IEEE 754 single precision bit pattern 0x3F000000 represents which value?

  1. 0.5
  2. 1.0
  3. 2.0
  4. 0.375
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Decoding a single-precision word means splitting it into sign, biased exponent and fraction, then applying (−1)s × 1.f × 2E−127.

Step by step.

  1. 0x3F000000 = 0011 1111 0000 0000 0000 0000 0000 0000.
  2. Sign bit 0, so the value is positive.
  3. Exponent field 0111 1110 = 126, so the true exponent is 126 − 127 = −1.
  4. Fraction field is all zeros, so the significand is 1.0.
  5. Value = 1.0 × 2−1 = 0.5.

Answer: 0.5

Common misconception. Subtracting 128 instead of 127, which shifts the answer by a factor of two and lands on 1.0 or 0.25.

Takeaway. Anchor yourself on three fixed words: 0x3F800000 is 1.0, 0x40000000 is 2.0, and halving subtracts one from the exponent field.

DL-U1-Q20NATmedium

Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. The value is (−1)s × (1 + f) × 2E−127, where f is the fraction field read as a binary fraction.

Step by step.

  1. 0x41A00000 = 0100 0001 1010 0000 0000 0000 0000 0000.
  2. Sign bit 0: positive.
  3. Exponent field 1000 0011 = 131, so the true exponent is 131 − 127 = 4.
  4. Fraction bits 010 followed by zeros: f = 0.25, so the significand is 1.25.
  5. Value = 1.25 × 24 = 1.25 × 16 = 20.

Answer: 20

Common misconception. Forgetting the implicit leading 1 and computing 0.25 × 16 = 4, or reading the fraction field as the integer 0xA0.

Takeaway. The implicit 1 is worth more than the whole fraction field: forgetting it is the most common single-precision decoding error.

DL-U1-Q21MCQhard

What is the smallest positive subnormal number representable in IEEE 754 single precision?

  1. 2−149
  2. 2−126
  3. 2−127
  4. 2−150
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. A subnormal number has an all-zero exponent field, which is decoded as the minimum normal exponent 1 − 127 = −126 with no implicit leading 1; the fraction then contributes directly.

Step by step.

  1. Take the exponent field as 0, which decodes to 2−126 as the scale.
  2. With the implicit 1 suppressed, the value is f × 2−126.
  3. The smallest positive fraction is the last bit set: f = 2−23.
  4. Value = 2−23 × 2−126 = 2−149, about 1.4 × 10−45.

Answer: 2−149

Common misconception. Answering 2−126, which is the smallest positive normal number; subnormals exist precisely to go below it.

Takeaway. Subnormals trade significand precision for range: the exponent stops decreasing and the leading 1 is dropped instead.

DL-U1-S2-Q14MCQeasy

An IEEE 754 single precision number devotes how many bits to the fraction field?

  1. 23
  2. 24
  3. 8
  4. 52
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Single precision is 1 sign bit + 8 exponent bits + 23 fraction bits = 32 bits.

Step by step.

  1. 32 − 1 − 8 = 23 bits remain for the fraction.
  2. The 24th significant bit is the implicit leading 1 that normalised numbers carry for free.

Answer: 23

Common misconception. Answering 24 counts the implicit leading 1, which is not stored; 52 is the double precision fraction width.

DL-U1-S2-Q15MCQeasy

The exponent bias used by IEEE 754 double precision is

  1. 127
  2. 1023
  3. 1024
  4. 2047
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. The bias of an excess-K exponent field with e bits is 2e−1 − 1.

Step by step.

  1. Double precision has an 11-bit exponent.
  2. 210 − 1 = 1024 − 1 = 1023.
  3. Single precision, with 8 exponent bits, uses 27 − 1 = 127.

Answer: 1023

Common misconception. 1024 is 2e−1 without the −1; 127 belongs to single precision.

DL-U1-S2-Q16MCQmedium

The 32-bit word 0x40400000 is an IEEE 754 single precision number. Its value is

  1. 3.0
  2. 1.5
  3. 6.0
  4. 2.5
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Decode the three fields: sign, then the biased exponent, then the fraction with an implicit leading 1.

Step by step.

  1. 0x40400000 = 0100 0000 0100 0000 0000 0000 0000 0000.
  2. Sign = 0, so the value is positive.
  3. Exponent = 1000 0000 = 128; unbiased = 128 − 127 = 1.
  4. Fraction = 100…0, so the significand is 1.12 = 1.5.
  5. Value = 1.5 × 21 = 3.0.

Answer: 3.0

Common misconception. Using the biased exponent 128 directly as a power of two, which gives an astronomically large value; the bias must be subtracted first.

DL-U1-S2-Q17MCQhard

The smallest positive normalised number representable in IEEE 754 single precision is

  1. 2−126
  2. 2−127
  3. 2−149
  4. 2−1022
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Normalised numbers have an exponent field from 1 to 254, so the smallest unbiased exponent is 1 − 127 = −126, with the smallest significand being exactly 1.0.

Step by step.

  1. Smallest legal exponent field for a normal number = 0000 0001 = 1.
  2. Unbiased exponent = 1 − 127 = −126.
  3. Smallest significand = 1.0 (fraction all zeros).
  4. Value = 1.0 × 2−126.

Answer: 2−126

Common misconception. 2−149 is the smallest positive subnormal, and 2−127 is not representable as a normal single precision number at all.

DL-U1-S2-Q18MSQmedium

Which of the following statements about IEEE 754 single precision are TRUE? (Select all that apply.)

  1. An all-zero exponent field with a non-zero fraction denotes a subnormal number.
  2. An all-one exponent field with an all-zero fraction denotes infinity.
  3. The format carries 23 fraction bits and therefore offers 23 bits of precision.
  4. An all-one exponent field with a non-zero fraction denotes NaN.
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. The two extreme exponent values are reserved: all-zero switches off the implicit leading 1, and all-one encodes the special values.

Step by step.

  1. (A) Exponent 0 with a non-zero fraction is subnormal, with no implicit leading 1. True.
  2. (B) Exponent 255 with fraction 0 is ±infinity according to the sign bit. True.
  3. (C) Precision is 24 bits: the 23 stored bits plus the implicit leading 1. False.
  4. (D) Exponent 255 with a non-zero fraction is NaN. True.

Answer: (A), (B) and (D)

Common misconception. Option (C) confuses the stored width with the precision; the implicit leading 1 is free information that every normalised number carries.

DL-U1-S2-Q19NAThard

Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Decode sign, biased exponent and fraction, then form (−1)s × 1.f × 2E−127.

Step by step.

  1. 0x41200000 = 0100 0001 0010 0000 0000 0000 0000 0000.
  2. Sign = 0 (positive).
  3. Exponent = 1000 0010 = 130; unbiased = 130 − 127 = 3.
  4. Fraction = 010…0, so the significand is 1.012 = 1.25.
  5. Value = 1.25 × 23 = 10.

Answer: 10

Common misconception. Forgetting the implicit leading 1 turns the significand into 0.25 and the answer into 2; the leading 1 is always present for normal numbers.

DL-U1-Q64MCQeasy

In IEEE 754 single precision, the value 1.0 is encoded as which 32-bit pattern?

  1. 0x3F000000
  2. 0x40000000
  3. 0x3F800000
  4. 0x3F7FFFFF
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. A single precision word is 1 sign bit, 8 exponent bits in excess-127 and 23 fraction bits; the leading 1 of a normal significand is implicit.

Step by step.

  1. Write 1.0 in the normalised form 1.0 × 20, so the fraction field is all zeros and the exponent is 0.
  2. Bias the exponent: 0 + 127 = 127 = 0111 1111.
  3. Assemble: sign 0, exponent 0111 1111, fraction 000 0000 0000 0000 0000 0000, giving 0011 1111 1000 0000 … = 0x3F800000.
  4. Sanity check on the neighbourhood: each unit in the exponent doubles the value, so 0x40000000 is 2.0 and 0x3F000000 is 0.5.

Answer: 0x3F800000, option (C).

Common misconception. Storing the exponent as the all-zeros field, which would make 1.0 look like 0x00000000, the pattern for zero. The field holds the biased exponent, so 1.0 carries the pattern of 127.

DL-U1-Q65MCQeasy

A floating-point format stores its exponent in IEEE 754 excess form. If the exponent field is k = 6 bits wide, the bias is

  1. 32
  2. 31
  3. 63
  4. 30
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. The IEEE 754 bias for a k-bit exponent field is 2k−1 − 1, which places the exponent range roughly symmetrically about zero while reserving the all-zeros and all-ones fields for subnormals, zero, infinity and NaN.

Step by step.

  1. With k = 6 the field holds 0 to 63.
  2. Bias = 25 − 1 = 32 − 1 = 31.
  3. The stored field e represents the true exponent e − 31, so the normal range runs from 1 − 31 = −30 to 62 − 31 = 31.
  4. Check against the known formats: k = 8 gives 127 and k = 11 gives 1023, both matching 2k−1 − 1.

Answer: 31, option (B).

Common misconception. Using 2k−1 = 32, which is a bias of the offset-binary kind but not the IEEE 754 value, or 2k − 1 = 63, which is the top of the field rather than the bias.

DL-U1-Q66MCQeasy

The 32-bit word 0x42C80000 is an IEEE 754 single precision number. Which decimal value does it represent?

  1. 100.0
  2. 50.0
  3. 200.0
  4. 12.5
Show answer & solution

Detailed solution

Concept. Decoding reverses the encoding: split the word into sign, biased exponent and fraction, unbias the exponent, then evaluate (−1)s × (1.fraction) × 2e.

Step by step.

  1. 0x42C80000 in binary is 0100 0010 1100 1000 …, so the sign is 0 and the exponent field is 1000 0101 = 133.
